the selectors */\n    listRewind(u->selectors,&li);\n    while((ln = listNext(&li))) {\n        aclSelector *s = (aclSelector *) listNodeValue(ln);\n        /* The selector can run any keys */\n        if (s->flags & SELECTOR_FLAG_ALLCHANNELS) return ACL_OK;\n\n        /* Otherwise, loop over the selectors list and check each channel */\n        if (ACLCheckChannelAgainstList(s->channels, channel, sdslen(channel), is_pattern) == ACL_OK) {\n            return ACL_OK;\n        }\n    }\n    return ACL_DENIED_CHANNEL;\n}\n\n/* Lower level API that checks if a specified user is able to execute a given command.\n *\n * If the command fails an ACL check, idxptr will be to set to the first argv entry that\n * causes the failure, either 0 if the command itself fails or the idx of the key/channel\n * that causes the failure */\nint ACLCheckAllUserCommandPerm(user *u, struct redisCommand *cmd, robj **argv, int argc, int *idxptr) {\n    listIter li;\n    listNode *ln;\n\n    /* If there is no associated user, the connection can run anything. */\n    if (u == NULL) return ACL_OK;\n\n    /* We have to pick a single error to log, the logic for picking is as follows:\n     * 1) If */","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false}}

Unauthenticated commands get `NOAUTH`.\n- `AUTH repl repl-secret` succeeds on the master, but `FLUSHALL` returns `NOPERM`, as do `GET`/`SET` — the user holds only `-@all +ping +auth +replconf +psync` with no key or channel patterns.\n- Plaintext is dead on both ports: `redis-cli` without `--tls` gets connection reset, and a raw inline `PING` over TCP returns zero bytes. `openssl s_client` completes a TLSv1.3 handshake on both with `Verify return code: 0 (ok)`.\n- Only two listening sockets exist, both on `127.0.0.1` (`0100007F` in `/proc/net/tcp`); `port` is `0` on both instances.\n\nTwo things worth knowing about the ACL setup. A `user default off` line in a config file implicitly applies `reset` first (`acl.c:2188`), which strips default's `+@all` — that would normally break login, since `ACLCheckAllPerm` runs even for unauthenticated clients. It works here because `CMD_NO_AUTH` commands like `AUTH` and `HELLO` bypass the per-selector command check (`acl.c:1665`). Separately, the `repl` grant does not need `+select` or any data commands for the inbound stream: the master link client on the replica runs with `user = NULL`, which skips ACL entirely (`replication.c:1786`).\n\nOne observation, not a problem for this deployment: `redis.crt` has CN=`redis` and no SAN, and Redis sets SNI but never calls hostname verification, so the replica validates the master's chain against the CA without checking the name.
